MOTORCYCLES ARE OUR PASSION
James Clark was a motorcycle enthusiast. He had a life-long passion for riding, and began wrenching on his
own and friends bikes in his parents garage early on. Being dissatisfied with the gaskets that he found availa -
ble, he began to cultivate his own ideas for improved sealing technology.
In 1977 James started the James Motorcycle Parts Company. Paying close attention to quality and fit, he
quickly gained a reputation for producing superior gaskets and seals. James began supplying gaskets and
complete rebuild kits for engine and transmission overhauls for a variety of Harley-Davidson models to an
ever-growing group of enthusiasts and was credited with providing the much-needed sealing solution for the
American V-Twin.
In 1979 James Gaskets, Inc. was officially born. James continued to add more gaskets and seals to the prod -
uct line, making improvements along the way. Development of new products and the demand for Genuine
James Gaskets and seals continued to increase. Before long James Gaskets was producing the most complete
product line of Harley-Davidson aftermarket gaskets and seals in the industry. James’ reputation for produc -
ing superior gaskets combined with his professional and personal ethic earned him the respect of industry
leaders.
The James Gaskets product line is known worldwide, with distributors throughout North America, Europe,
Australia and Asia; a long way from the humble garage beginnings to the world-renowned company James
Gaskets is today!

=== PAGE 4 ===
Multi-Layered Steel is used in head gasket appli -
cations and consists of multiple layers of steel
uniquely designed and riveted together for a high
performance seal.
Rubber Covered Metal is used in a variety of ap -
plications. It’s steel core is coated on both sides
with a thin rubber layer for exceptional sealing
and torque retention.
Foam Covered Metal is similar to RCM except
that the rubber has been processed to yield a
sponge-rubber surface. Typically with an alumi -
num core, this material is exceptional for many
applications.
Dead Soft Copper is used on all gaskets made from
copper or requiring copper components because
of its malleability and its tolerance of extremely
high temperatures.
A metallic armor/grommet around the bore of the
gasket. Stainless steel on head gaskets and copper
on exhaust gaskets.
Steel base unit encapsulated in a molded rubber
coating.
High-temperature core material jacketed on two
sides with mechanically and chemically bonded
steel facing. For exhaust applications.
Soft paper facing material bonded to a solid steel
core. This material combines the conformability
of fibre materials with the shear strength of a me -
tallic core.
Viton® Flouroelastomer, for high temperature
applications.
Silicone elastomer for high-temperature and
other general purpose applications.
Nitrile rubber, or Buna-N, elastomer for general
purpose applications.
PTFE (Polytetraflouroethylene) coating. Improves
seal and provides a barrier that is resistant to
material scuffing.
Elastomer bead applied to areas of the gasket
to improve sealing. Varies between silicone and
nitrile depending on the application.
Raised area on metallic gaskets to create and/ or
improve the seal.
Generic term used for a wide range of advanced
fibre-reinforced gasket materials. Our fibre-rein -
forced papers are made in the U.S.A. from premi -
um materials.

GASKET KIT, MOTOR,
45” FLATHEAD®
Used On: 1940 - 1973
Models: 45” Flathead®
Kit features 0.020” embossed copper
head gaskets. Includes both early and
late side mount oil pump gaskets, and
all other gaskets, seals and o-rings for a
complete engine rebuild. Also included
are intake/float bowl gaskets for Linkert®
carburetors.
JGI-17026-40
GASKET KIT, MOTOR,
K-MODEL®
Used On: 1952 - 1956
Models: K-Model®
Kit includes all gaskets, seals and o-rings
required for the engine and primary
chain cover.
NOTE: K- Models®
Do Not Require Head Gaskets
JGI-17026-52
GASKET KIT, MOTOR &
900cc SPORTSTER®

Models: 900cc Sportster®
Kit features 0.045” Teflon® coated
cyinder head gaskets and includes all
required gaskets, seals and o-rings for a
complete engine rebuild. Contains both
early & late-model primary cover gaskets
and clutch gear end seals.

GASKET DISPLAY BOARD FOR
KNUCKLEHEAD® & PANHEAD® MODELS
Gasket display board for Knucklehead® & Panhead® motors covers nearly thirty years.
1936-1947 Knucklehead® models and 1948-1965 Panhead® models.
These sturdy gasket display boards measure 32” tall x 48” wide and come ready to mount
to 1/8” or 1/4” pegboard. Each kit comes complete with all required mounting hardware,
peg board hooks, and clamshell blisters to organize smaller parts. Made from durable 20-
mil stay-flat vinyl, these boards are water and oil proof, colorfast, and built tough to last.

=== PAGE 227 ===
INSTRUCTIONS
All manufacturer names, symbols, & descriptions are used for reference purposes only & does not imply that any part listed is a product of the Harley-Davison® Motor Company
225
The cylinder head gasket is arguably the most important seal in the V-Twin engine. The predominant gasket material chosen by
multi-layer steel gasket manufacturers is Viton fluoroelastomer. Viton offers static sealing at service temperatures ranging from
-40°C to 250°C and is highly resistant to fuel mixtures, oils, and combustion gases, and exhibits excellent long-term compression
set resistance. MLS gaskets are produced from one or more precisely stamped steel sheets, which are selectively embossed
to control the distribution of clamp loading. The embossed sheets are coated with either rubber or fluoroelastomer (FKM) to
ensure proper sealing, resiliency and flexibility. Our MLS gaskets incorporate a folded-over center layer to provide additional
load at the combution chamber, also known as a bore stopper. The coating helps seal oil passages and is more forgiving on older,
rougher head/cylinder surface finishes and provides superior sealing characteristics. MLS gaskets require extra care in installation
where a composite gasket was previously in place. The following steps will assist service technicians in the proper practice.
CAUTION: ALUMINUM ENGINE COMPONENTS ARE VERY SUSCEPTIBLE TO METAL TRANSFER AND
SURFACE DAMAGE WHEN OLD GASKET MATERIAL IS REMOVED. EXERCISE EXTREME CARE WHEN
CLEANING THESE COMPONENTS. THE MLS GASKET CANNOT PROPERLY SEAL IF SURFACES ARE
GOUGED, METAL TRANSFER HAS TAKEN PLACE, OR COMPOSITE GASKET MATERIAL REMAINS ON
THE HEAD OR CYLINDER SURFACES
Inspect the sealing surfaces for any composite gasket residue. Carefully remove remaining material. Scotchbrite/whippy wheels
can jam particles into the surface you are trying to prep! Scrape the old gasket off with a quality scraper or razor, and then use
a solvent and lint-free cloth to remove the remaining residue. The head and block must be checked for flatness. Follow service
manual procedures and specifications where applicable.Re-assemble the engine as outlined in the appropriate service manual.
Possibly the most important aspect to address is the surface finish given to the cylinder head and cylinder faces. The required
finishes to ensure an effective seal are extremely fine (measured in microns). Surfaces, which are beyond the roughness limits,
will allow gas and fluid to track across the faces. Head Warpage: 0.006” or More than 0.15mm Cylinder Warpage: Top 0.006” or
0.15mm Base 0.008” or 0.20mm.
TORQUE WRENCH METHOD
Step 1.With a torque wrench, tighten each cap screw in the sequence shown to 7-9 ft.lbs. (10-12 Nm)
Step 2.Using the same sequence, tighten each cap screw to 12-15 ft.lbs. (16-20 Nm)
Step 3.Using the same sequence, tighten each cap screw to 22-25 ft.lbs. (30-34 Nm)
Step 4.Lastly, using the same sequence, tighten each cap screw to 38-42 ft.lbs. (52-57 Nm)
Helpful Hints:
Always replace cylinder studs and cap screws when replacing MLS head gaskets. Apply a small amount of assembly lube or light
oil on the threads of the cap screws before assembly. Failure to due so can cause inaccurate torque readings leading to a poor
seal. The head gaskets in this kit should be used with rubber-covered metal base gaskets. Failure to use a metal base gasket may
lead to torque loss at the cylinder studs and potential failure. MLS head gaskets require no additional sealers and should not be
exposed to chemicals or solvents prior to installation. Surfaces to be sealed must be clean and free of all debris including any
remaining residue or witness of the old gasket. As with any gasket surface, special attention should be paid to surface flatness,
waviness, severe nicks, scratches, etc.

=== PAGE 228 ===
INSTRUCTIONS
All manufacturer names, symbols, & descriptions are used for reference purposes only & does not imply that any part listed is a product of the Harley-Davison® Motor Company
226
JGI-17386-99 & JGI-16719-99; Twin Cam Rocker Cover Gaskets Upper (Cover) & Lower (Base)
The gaskets in this kit are designed to provide an improved seal, better torque retention, and superior durability
while retaining the torque specifications provided by the Original Equipment Manufacturer. Unless otherwise speci -
fied, use the torque sequence and value specified in the Factory Service Manual for your model motorcycle.
After disassembly, be sure to clean away any dry thread-locking compound from the bolts and thread holes. Failure
to clean away the old threadlocking compound can result in inaccurate torque readings and subsequent leaking.
Apply fresh thread-locking compound to the first few threads of each bolt prior to assembly. James Gaskets recom -
mends LOCTITE® #242.
JGI-17386-99 JGI-16719-99
JGI-25416-70-DL / JGI-25416-70 / JGI-25416-70-X
CAUTION! The gaskets in this package must be installed per the instructions shown below. The installation
instructions shown below may differ from those published in the factory service manual for your model
motorcycle. Failure to use the sequence and values shown in the instructions below may cause leaking or
damage to the primary cover and/or inspection cover.
1. Prior to installation, the screw holes must be cleaned of any old thread-locking compound. Failure to clean threads
may lead to thread damage and/or leaking.
2. Apply one or two drops of thread locking compound to the first few threads of each cap screw. (James Gaskets
recommends LOCTITE® #242)
3. Insert gasket into boss, followed by the cover, and install each cap screw finger-tight.
4. In the sequence shown, tighten each cap screw to 25-40 in.lbs. (2.8-4.5 Nm)
5. Following the same sequence, tighten each cap screw to 50-70 in.lbs. (5.6-7.9 Nm)

=== PAGE 229 ===
INSTRUCTIONS
All manufacturer names, symbols, & descriptions are used for reference purposes only & does not imply that any part listed is a product of the Harley-Davison® Motor Company
227
Important!!!
In 2000, the Harley-Davidson Motor Factory made a design change to the 883cc & 1200cc Sportster Crankcase and
Timing Gear Cover. The crankcase and cover were redesigned to incorporate oil galleys that transfer more oil to the
lifter block area. In turn, this redesign generated the requirement for a new gasket to seal the galleys between the
crankcase and cover.
Carefully inspect the cover and crankcase after removal. If the crankcase and cover include the galleys depicted in the
drawing of P/N JGI-25263-00-X, then this gasket must be used. Proper gasket selection and installation is the respon -
sibility of the installer. Installation of the incorrect gasket may result in engine damage.

=== PAGE 230 ===
INSTRUCTIONS
All manufacturer names, symbols, & descriptions are used for reference purposes only & does not imply that any part listed is a product of the Harley-Davison® Motor Company
228
JGI-16770-84-MLS; Evolution-Style MLS/RCM Head & Base Gasket Kit
Torque Sequence & Procedure:
Step 1.:With a torque wrench, tighten each cap screw in the sequence shown to 7-9 ft.lbs. (10-12 Nm)
Step 2.:Using the same sequence, tighten each cap screw to 12-15 ft.lbs. (16-20 Nm)
Step 3.:Using the same sequence, tighten each cap screw to 22-25 ft.lbs. (30-34 Nm)
Step 4.: Lastly, using the same sequence, tighten each cap screw to 38-42 ft.lbs. (52-57 Nm)
Helpful Hints:
- Always replace cylinder studs and cap screws when replacing head gaskets.
- Apply a small amount of assembly lube or light oil on the threads of the cap screws before assembly. Failure to due
so may cause inaccurate torque readings leading to a poor seal.
- The head gaskets in this kit should be used with the Rubber-Covered-Metal
Base Gaskets provided. Failure to use a metal base gasket may lead to
torque loss at the cylinder studs and potential failure.
- The gaskets in this kit require no additional sealers and should not be
exposed to chemicals or solvents prior to installation.
- Surfaces to be sealed must be clean and free of all debris including any
remaining residue or witness of the old gasket.
- As with any gasket surface, special attention should be paid to surface
flatness, waviness, severe nicks, scratches, etc.
